src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/db/Coat_of_Arms_of_Josemar%C3%ADa_Escriv%C3%A1_%28Order_of_Charles_III%29.svg/150px-Coat_of_Arms_of_Josemar%C3%ADa_Escriv%C3%A1_%28Order_of_Charles_III%29.svg.png" decoding="async" width="150" height="142" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/db/Coat_of_Arms_of_Josemar%C3%ADa_Escriv%C3%A1_%28Order_of_Charles_III%29.svg/225px-Coat_of_Arms_of_Josemar%C3%ADa_Escriv%C3%A1_%28Order_of_Charles_III%29.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/db/Coat_of_Arms_of_Josemar%C3%ADa_Escriv%C3%A1_%28Order_of_Charles_III%29.svg/300px-Coat_of_Arms_of_Josemar%C3%ADa_Escriv%C3%A1_%28Order_of_Charles_III%29.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="966" data-file-height="912" /></a><br />Coat of arms of Saint Josemaria Escrivá</td></tr></tbody></table>
<p><b>Josemaría Escrivá de Balaguer y Albás</b> (9 January 1902&#160;– 26 June 1975) was a Spanish <a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholicism" class="mw-redirect" title="Roman Catholicism">Roman Catholic</a> priest. He founded <a href="/wiki/Opus_Dei" title="Opus Dei">Opus Dei</a>, an organization of <a href="/wiki/Catholic_Laity" class="mw-redirect" title="Catholic Laity">laypeople</a> and <a href="/wiki/Priesthood_(Catholic_Church)" class="mw-redirect" title="Priesthood (Catholic Church)">priests</a> dedicated to the teaching that everyone is called to holiness by God and to discover sanctity in their ordinary lives.<sup id="cite_ref-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-1">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup> He was <a href="/wiki/Canonization" title="Canonization">canonized</a> in 2002 by <a href="/wiki/Pope_John_Paul_II" title="Pope John Paul II">Pope John Paul II</a>, who declared Josemaría should be "counted among the great witnesses of Christianity."
</p><p>Escrivá gained a doctorate in <a href="/wiki/Civil_law_(legal_system)" title="Civil law (legal system)">civil law</a> at the <a href="/wiki/Complutense_University_of_Madrid" title="Complutense University of Madrid">Complutense University of Madrid</a> and a doctorate in <a href="/wiki/Theology" title="Theology">theology</a> at the <a href="/wiki/Pontifical_Lateran_University" title="Pontifical Lateran University">Lateran University</a> in Rome. His principal work was the initiation, government and expansion of Opus Dei. Escrivá's best-known publication is <i><a href="/wiki/The_Way_(book)" title="The Way (book)">The Way</a></i>, which has been translated into 43 languages and has sold several million copies.
</p><p>Escrivá and Opus Dei have been accused of secrecy, <a href="/wiki/Elitism" title="Elitism">elitism</a>, <a href="/wiki/Cult" title="Cult">cult</a>-like practices, and involvement with <a href="/wiki/Right-wing_politics" title="Right-wing politics">right-wing</a> causes, such as the <a href="/wiki/Francoist_State" class="mw-redirect" title="Francoist State">rule of Francisco Franco</a> in Spain (1939–1975).<sup id="cite_ref-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-2">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-3">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup> After his death, his canonization attracted considerable attention and controversy among some Catholics and the worldwide press.<sup id="cite_ref-Woodward-Book_4-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Woodward-Book-4">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup> Several journalists who have investigated the history of Opus Dei, among them Vatican analyst <a href="/wiki/John_L._Allen_Jr." title="John L. Allen Jr.">John L. Allen Jr.</a>, have argued that many of these accusations are unproven or have grown from allegations by enemies of Escrivá and his organization.<sup id="cite_ref-whitehouse_5-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-whitehouse-5">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Noam_6-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Noam-6">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-7">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup> Cardinal Albino Luciani (later <a href="/wiki/Pope_John_Paul_I" title="Pope John Paul I">Pope John Paul I</a>),<sup id="cite_ref-A._Luciani_1978_8-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-A._Luciani_1978-8">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup> John Paul II, <a href="/wiki/Benedict_XVI" class="mw-redirect" title="Benedict XVI">Benedict XVI</a>, <a href="/wiki/Pope_Francis" title="Pope Francis">Pope Francis</a>, <a href="/wiki/%C3%93scar_Romero" title="Óscar Romero">Óscar Romero</a>, and many other Catholic leaders have endorsed Escrivá's teaching concerning the <a href="/wiki/Universal_call_to_holiness" title="Universal call to holiness">universal call to holiness</a>, the role of the <a href="/wiki/Laity" title="Laity">laity</a>, and the sanctifying effect of ordinary work.<sup id="cite_ref-Official_Papal_9-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Official_Papal-9">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup> According to Allen, among Catholics, Escrivá is "reviled by some and venerated by millions more".<sup id="cite_ref-ALLENBOOOK_10-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ALLENBOOOK-10">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Biography">Biography</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Josemar%C3%ADa_Escriv%C3%A1&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: Biography">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Early_life">Early life</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Josemar%C3%ADa_Escriv%C3%A1&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: Early life">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p><b>José María Mariano Escriva y Albás</b> was born to José Escriva y Corzán and his wife, María de los Dolores Albás y Blanc on 9 January 1902, in the small town of <a href="/wiki/Barbastro" title="Barbastro">Barbastro</a>, in <a href="/wiki/Huesca_(province)" class="mw-redirect" title="Huesca (province)">Huesca, Spain</a>, the second of six children and the first of two sons. José Escrivá was a merchant and a partner in a textile company that eventually went bankrupt, forcing the family to relocate in 1915 to the city of <a href="/wiki/Logro%C3%B1o" title="Logroño">Logroño</a>, in the northern province of <a href="/wiki/La_Rioja_(Spain)" class="mw-redirect" title="La Rioja (Spain)">La Rioja</a>, where he worked as a clerk in a clothing store.<sup id="cite_ref-11" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-11">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup> Young Josemaría first felt that "he had been chosen for something", it is reported, when he saw footprints left in the snow by a <a href="/wiki/Monk" title="Monk">monk</a> walking barefoot.<sup id="cite_ref-VDP_12-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-VDP-12">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-13" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-13">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>With his father's blessing, Escrivá prepared to become a <a href="/wiki/Priest" title="Priest">priest</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Catholic_Church" title="Catholic Church">Catholic Church</a>.  He studied first in Logroño and then in <a href="/wiki/Zaragoza" title="Zaragoza">Zaragoza</a>, where he was ordained as deacon on Saturday, 20 December 1924. He was ordained a <a href="/wiki/Priest" title="Priest">priest</a>, also in Zaragoza, on Saturday, 28 March 1925. After a brief appointment to a rural parish in <a href="/wiki/Perdiguera" title="Perdiguera">Perdiguera</a>, he went to <a href="/wiki/Madrid" title="Madrid">Madrid</a>, the Spanish capital, in 1927 to study <a href="/wiki/Law" title="Law">law</a> at the <a href="/wiki/Complutense_University_of_Madrid" title="Complutense University of Madrid">Central University</a>. In Madrid, Escrivá was employed as a private tutor and as a <a href="/wiki/Chaplain" title="Chaplain">chaplain</a> to the Foundation of Santa Isabel, which comprised the royal <a href="/wiki/Convent_of_Santa_Isabel" title="Convent of Santa Isabel">Convent of Santa Isabel</a> and a school managed by the <a href="/wiki/Little_Sisters_of_the_Assumption" title="Little Sisters of the Assumption">Little Sisters of the Assumption</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-14" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-14">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Mission_as_the_founder_of_Opus_Dei">Mission as the founder of Opus Dei</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Josemar%C3%ADa_Escriv%C3%A1&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: Mission as the founder of Opus Dei">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>A prayerful retreat helped him to discern more definitely what he considered to be God's will for him, and, on 2 October 1928, he "saw" <a href="/wiki/Opus_Dei" title="Opus Dei">Opus Dei</a> (English: <span lang="en">Work of God</span>), a way by which Catholics might learn to sanctify themselves by their secular work.<sup id="cite_ref-Burger_15-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Burger-15">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> He founded it in 1928, and Pius XII gave it final approval in 1950. According to the decree of the <a href="/wiki/Congregation_for_the_Causes_of_Saints" class="mw-redirect" title="Congregation for the Causes of Saints">Congregation for the Causes of Saints</a>, which contains a condensed biography of Escrivá, "[t]o this mission he gave himself totally. From the beginning his was a very wide-ranging apostolate in social environments of all kinds. He worked especially among the poor and the sick languishing in the slums and hospitals of Madrid."<sup id="cite_ref-ACF_16-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ACF-16">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>During the <a href="/wiki/Spanish_Civil_War" title="Spanish Civil War">Spanish Civil War</a>, Escrivá fled from Madrid, which was controlled by the anti-clerical <a href="/wiki/Republican_faction_(Spanish_Civil_War)" title="Republican faction (Spanish Civil War)">Republicans</a>, via <a href="/wiki/Andorra" title="Andorra">Andorra</a> and <a href="/wiki/France" title="France">France</a>, to the city of <a href="/wiki/Burgos" title="Burgos">Burgos</a>, which was the headquarters of General <a href="/wiki/Francisco_Franco" title="Francisco Franco">Francisco Franco</a>'s <a href="/wiki/Nationalist_faction_(Spanish_Civil_War)" title="Nationalist faction (Spanish Civil War)">Nationalist</a> forces.<sup id="cite_ref-17" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-17">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup> After the war ended in 1939 with Franco's victory, Escrivá was able to resume his studies in Madrid and complete a doctorate in law, for which he submitted a thesis on the historical jurisdiction of the <a href="/wiki/Abbess" title="Abbess">Abbess</a> of <a href="/wiki/Abbey_of_Santa_Mar%C3%ADa_la_Real_de_Las_Huelgas" title="Abbey of Santa María la Real de Las Huelgas">Santa María la Real de Las Huelgas</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Rocca_18-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Rocca-18">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Priestly Society of the Holy Cross, affiliated with Opus Dei, was founded on Sunday, 14 February 1943.  Escrivá relocated to <a href="/wiki/Rome" title="Rome">Rome</a> in 1946. The decree declaring Escrivá "Venerable" states that "in 1947 and on Monday, 16 June 1950, he obtained approval of Opus Dei as an institution of pontifical right. With tireless charity and operative hope he guided the development of Opus Dei throughout the world, activating a vast mobilization of lay people ... He gave life to numerous initiatives in the work of evangelization and human welfare; he fostered vocations to the priesthood and the religious life everywhere... Above all, he devoted himself tirelessly to the task of forming the members of Opus Dei."<sup id="cite_ref-ACF_16-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ACF-16">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Later_years">Later years</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Josemar%C3%ADa_Escriv%C3%A1&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Later years">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>According to some accounts, at the age of two he suffered from a disease (perhaps <a href="/wiki/Epilepsy" title="Epilepsy">epilepsy</a><sup id="cite_ref-19" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-19">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup>) so severe that the doctors expected him to die soon, but his mother had taken him to <a href="/wiki/Torreciudad" title="Torreciudad">Torreciudad</a>, where the Aragonese locals venerated a statue of the <a href="/wiki/Mary_(mother_of_Jesus)" class="mw-redirect" title="Mary (mother of Jesus)">Virgin Mary</a> (as "Our Lady of the Angels"), thought to date from the 11th century. Escrivá recovered and, as the director of Opus Dei during the 1960s and 1970s, promoted and oversaw the design and construction of a major shrine at Torreciudad. The new shrine was inaugurated on 7 July 1975, soon after Escrivá's death, and to this day remains the spiritual center of Opus Dei, as well as an important destination for pilgrimage.<sup id="cite_ref-20" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-20">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup> By the time of Escrivá's death in 1975, the members of Opus Dei numbered some 60,000 in 80 countries.<sup id="cite_ref-21" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-21">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup> As an adult, Escrivá suffered from <a href="/wiki/Diabetes_mellitus_type_1" class="mw-redirect" title="Diabetes mellitus type 1">type 1 diabetes</a><sup id="cite_ref-22" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-22">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup> and, according to some sources, also epilepsy.<sup id="cite_ref-23" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-23">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1950, Escrivá was appointed an <a href="/wiki/Honorary_prelate#Reform" class="mw-redirect" title="Honorary prelate">Honorary Domestic Prelate</a> by Pope <a href="/wiki/Pius_XII" class="mw-redirect" title="Pius XII">Pius XII</a>, which allowed him to use the title of <a href="/wiki/Monsignor" title="Monsignor">Monsignor</a>. In 1955, he received a doctorate of theology from the <a href="/wiki/Pontifical_Lateran_University" title="Pontifical Lateran University">Pontifical Lateran University</a> in Rome.<sup id="cite_ref-Rocca_18-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Rocca-18">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup> He was a consultor to two Vatican congregations (the Congregation for Seminaries and Universities and the Pontifical Commission for the Authentic Interpretation of the Code of <a href="/wiki/Canon_law_(Catholic_Church)" class="mw-redirect" title="Canon law (Catholic Church)">Canon Law</a>) and an honorary member of the Pontifical Academy of Theology. The <a href="/wiki/Second_Vatican_Council" title="Second Vatican Council">Second Vatican Council</a> (1962–65) confirmed the importance of the <a href="/wiki/Universal_call_to_holiness" title="Universal call to holiness">universal call to holiness</a>, the role of the laity, and the Mass as the basis of Christian life.<sup id="cite_ref-24" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-24">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1948 Escrivá founded the Collegium Romanum Sanctae Crucis (Roman College of the Holy Cross), Opus Dei's educational center for men, in Rome. In 1953 he founded the Collegium Romanum Sanctae Mariae (Roman College of Saint Mary) to serve the women's section (these institutions are now joined into the <a href="/wiki/Pontifical_University_of_the_Holy_Cross" title="Pontifical University of the Holy Cross">Pontifical University of the Holy Cross</a>.) Escrivá also established the <a href="/wiki/University_of_Navarre" class="mw-redirect" title="University of Navarre">University of Navarre</a>, in <a href="/wiki/Pamplona" title="Pamplona">Pamplona</a>, and the <a href="/wiki/University_of_Piura" title="University of Piura">University of Piura</a> (in <a href="/wiki/Peru" title="Peru">Peru</a>), as secular institutions affiliated with Opus Dei. Escrivá died of cardiac arrest on 26 June 1975, aged 73.
</p><p>Three years after Escrivá died, the then Cardinal Albino Luciani (later <a href="/wiki/Pope_John_Paul_I" title="Pope John Paul I">Pope John Paul I</a>) celebrated the originality of his contribution to Christian spirituality.<sup id="cite_ref-A._Luciani_1978_8-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-A._Luciani_1978-8">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Personality_and_attitudes">Personality and attitudes</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Josemar%C3%ADa_Escriv%C3%A1&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: Personality and attitudes">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Attitudes_in_general">Attitudes in general</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Josemar%C3%ADa_Escriv%C3%A1&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: Attitudes in general">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>One of the persons who knew Escrivá best was the Bishop of Madrid, where Opus Dei was initiated, Bishop Leopoldo Eijo y Garay, for Escrivá would visit and report to him quite frequently and the two established a very strong friendship. In a 1943 report to Rome, the bishop stated: "The distinctive notes of his character are his energy and his capacity for organization and government; with an ability to pass unnoticed. He has shown himself most obedient to the Church hierarchy -- one very special hallmark of his priestly work is the way he fosters, in speech and in writing, in public and in private, love for Holy Mother Church and for the Roman Pontiff." Bishop Eijo y Garay wrote to the Jesuit Provincial of Toledo, Carlos Gomez Martinho, S.J. in 1941: "Fr. Escrivá is an exemplary priest, chosen by God for apostolic enterprises; humble, prudent, self-sacrificing in work, docile to his bishop, of outstanding intelligence and with a very solid spiritual and doctrinal formation." Eijo y Garay told an officer of the <a href="/wiki/Falange" class="mw-redirect" title="Falange">Falange</a> that "[T]o think that Fr. Josemaría Escrivá is capable of creating anything secret is absurd. He is as frank and open as a child!"<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2011)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Viktor_Frankl" title="Viktor Frankl">Viktor Frankl</a>, an Austrian psychiatrist and neurologist, founder of "<a href="/wiki/Logotherapy" title="Logotherapy">logotherapy</a>", and a Nazi concentration camp survivor, met Escrivá in Rome in 1970 and later wrote of "the refreshing serenity which emanated from him and warmed the whole conversation", and "the unbelievable rhythm" with which his thought flowed, and finally "his amazing capacity" for getting into "immediate contact" with those with whom he was speaking. Frankl went on: "Escrivá evidently lived totally in the present moment, he opened out to it completely, and gave himself entirely to it."<sup id="cite_ref-25" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-25">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-26" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-26">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>According to <a href="/wiki/%C3%81lvaro_del_Portillo" title="Álvaro del Portillo">Álvaro del Portillo</a>, who was Escrivá's closest collaborator for many years, there was one basic quality of Escrivá "that pervaded everything else: his dedication to God, and to all souls for God's sake; his constant readiness to correspond generously to the will of God."<sup id="cite_ref-del_Portillo_1996_27-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-del_Portillo_1996-27">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup> Pope <a href="/wiki/Paul_VI" class="mw-redirect" title="Paul VI">Paul VI</a> summarized his opinion of what he termed the "extraordinariness" of Escrivá's sanctity in this way: "He is one of those men who has received the most charisms (supernatural gifts) and have corresponded most generously to them."
</p><p>"The first impression one gets from watching Escrivá 'live'", <a href="/wiki/John_L._Allen_Jr." title="John L. Allen Jr.">John L. Allen Jr.</a> writes after watching some movies on the founder of Opus Dei in 2005, "is his effervescence, his keen sense of humor. He cracks jokes, makes faces, roams the stage, and generally leaves his audience in stitches in off-the-cuff responses to questions from people in the crowd."<sup id="cite_ref-28" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-28">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Critics, such as Spanish architect <a href="/wiki/Miguel_Fisac" title="Miguel Fisac">Miguel Fisac</a>, who was one of the earliest members of Opus Dei and who associated with Escrivá for nearly twenty years before ending his relation with Escrivá and Opus Dei, have given a very different description of Escrivá as a pious but vain, secretive, and ambitious man, given to private displays of violent temper, and who demonstrated little charity towards others or genuine concern for the poor.<sup id="cite_ref-Fisac1_29-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Fisac1-29">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup> According to British journalist <a href="/wiki/Giles_Tremlett" title="Giles Tremlett">Giles Tremlett</a>, "biographies of Escrivá have produced conflicting visions of the saint as either a loving, caring charismatic person or a mean-spirited, manipulative egoist".<sup id="cite_ref-30" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-30">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup> French historian Édouard de Blaye has referred to Escrivá as a "mixture of mysticism and ambition".<sup id="cite_ref-31" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-31">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Towards_God">Towards God</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Josemar%C3%ADa_Escriv%C3%A1&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: Towards God">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Prayer">Prayer</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Josemar%C3%ADa_Escriv%C3%A1&amp;action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: Prayer">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>On the centennial of Escrivá's birthday, Cardinal Ratzinger (who became <a href="/wiki/Pope_Benedict_XVI" title="Pope Benedict XVI">Pope Benedict XVI</a>) commented: "I have always been impressed by Josemaría Escrivá's explanation of the name 'Opus Dei': an explanation ... gives us an idea of the founder's spiritual profile. Escrivá knew he had to found something, but he was also conscious that what he was founding was not his own work, that he himself did not invent anything and that the Lord was merely making use of him. So it was not his work, but Opus Dei (God's Work). [This] gives us to understand that he was in a permanent dialogue, a real contact with the One who created us and works for us and with us... If therefore St Josemaría speaks of the common vocation to holiness, it seems to me that he is basically drawing on his own personal experience, not of having done incredible things himself, but of having let God work. Therefore a renewal, a force for good was born in the world even if human weaknesses will always remain."<sup id="cite_ref-32" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-32">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In his canonization homily, Pope John Paul II described Escrivá as "a master in the practice of prayer, which he considered to be an extraordinary 'weapon' to redeem the world...It is not a paradox but a perennial truth; the fruitfulness of the apostolate lies above all in prayer and in intense and constant sacramental life." In John Paul II's <i>Decree of Canonization</i>, he refers to the five brief prayers or aspirations of Escrivá through which "one can trace the entire life story of Blessed Josemaría Escrivá. He was barely sixteen when he began to recite the first two aspirations [<i>Domine, ut videam!</i>, Lord, that I might see! and <i>Domina, ut sit!</i>, Lady, that it might be!], as soon as he had the first inklings of God's call. They expressed the burning desire of his heart: to see what God was asking of him, so that he might do it without delay, lovingly fulfilling the Lord's will.<sup id="cite_ref-33" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-33">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup> The third aspiration [<i>Omnes cum Petro ad Iesum per Mariam!</i>, All together with Peter to Jesus through Mary!] appears frequently in his writings as a young priest and shows how his zeal to win souls for God went hand in hand with both a firm determination to be faithful to the Church and an ardent devotion to Mary, the Virgin Mother of God. <i>Regnare Christum volumus!</i> We want Christ to reign!:<sup id="cite_ref-34" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-34">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup> these words aptly express his constant pastoral concern to spread among all men and women the call to share, through Christ, in the dignity of God's children. God's sons and daughters should live for the purpose, to serve Him alone: <i>Deo omnis gloria!</i> All the glory to God!"<sup id="cite_ref-35" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-35">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-36" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-36">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>During the thanksgiving Mass for the canonization of St. Josemaría, <a href="/wiki/John_Paul_II" class="mw-redirect" title="John Paul II">John Paul II</a>, said: "In the Founder of Opus Dei, there is an extraordinary love for the will of God. There exists a sure criterion of holiness: fidelity in accomplishing the divine will down to the last consequences. For each one of us the Lord has a plan, to each he entrusts a mission on earth. The saint could not even conceive of himself outside of God's plan. He lived only to achieve it. St Josemaría was chosen by the Lord to announce the universal call to holiness and to point out that daily life and ordinary activities are a path to holiness. One could say that he was the saint of ordinary life."<sup id="cite_ref-JPII-2002_37-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-JPII-2002-37">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>
Not all Catholic commentators, however, were impressed equally by Escrivá's spirituality.  For instance, the <a href="/wiki/Switzerland" title="Switzerland">Swiss</a> theologian <a href="/wiki/Hans_Urs_von_Balthasar" title="Hans Urs von Balthasar">Hans Urs von Balthasar</a> wrote in an article of 1963 that Escrivá's <i>The Way</i> provided an "insufficient spirituality" to sustain a religious organization and that the book was hardly more than "a little Spanish manual for advanced <a href="/wiki/Scout_(Scouting)" title="Scout (Scouting)">Boy Scouts</a>".<sup id="cite_ref-Balthasar_38-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Balthasar-38">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup> Von Balthasar also questioned the attitudes towards prayer described by <i>The Way</i>, declaring that Escrivá's use of prayer <style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r996844942">.mw-parser-output .templatequote{overflow:hidden;margin:1em 0;padding:0 40px}.mw-parser-output .templatequote .templatequotecite{line-height:1.5em;text-align:left;padding-left:1.6em;margin-top:0}</style></p><blockquote class="templatequote"><p>moves almost exclusively within the circle of the self, of a self that must be great and strong, equipped with <a href="/wiki/Paganism" title="Paganism">pagan</a> virtues, apostolic and <a href="/wiki/Napoleon" title="Napoleon">Napoleonic</a>. That which is most necessary, which is the contemplative rooting of the <a href="/wiki/Logos_(Christianity)" title="Logos (Christianity)">Word</a> "on good soil" (Matthew 13:8), that which constitutes the aim of the prayers of the saints, of the great founders, the prayer of a <a href="/wiki/Charles_de_Foucauld" title="Charles de Foucauld">Foucauld</a>, is something one will search for in vain here.<sup id="cite_ref-Balthasar_38-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Balthasar-38">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup></p></blockquote>
<p>Von Balthasar repeated his negative evaluation of <i>The Way</i> in a television interview from 1984.<sup id="cite_ref-Allen_2005_64_39-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Allen_2005_64-39">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Similar criticism of Escrivá's spirituality has been stated by other commentators: for instance, according to <a href="/wiki/Kenneth_L._Woodward" title="Kenneth L. Woodward">Kenneth L. Woodward</a>, a journalist who specializes in articles about the Catholic Church, "to judge by his writings alone, Escrivá's was an unexceptional spirit, derivative and often banal in his thoughts, personally inspiring, perhaps, but devoid of original insights", whose book <i>The Way</i> reveals "a remarkable narrowness of mind, weariness of human sexuality, and artlessness of expression."<sup id="cite_ref-Woodward-Book-TheWay_40-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Woodward-Book-TheWay-40">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Towards_the_liturgy">Towards the liturgy</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Josemar%C3%ADa_Escriv%C3%A1&amp;action=edit&amp;section=9" title="Edit section: Towards the liturgy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>Escrivá conceived the Mass as the "Source and summit of the Christian's interior life," a terminology which was later used by the <a href="/wiki/Second_Vatican_Council" title="Second Vatican Council">Second Vatican Council</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-VDP_12-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-VDP-12">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup> Escrivá strove to obey whatever was indicated by the competent authority regarding the celebration of Mass and "[h]e took all necessary steps to ensure that the prescriptions of Vatican II, notably in the area of the liturgy, were applied within Opus Dei."<sup id="cite_ref-41" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-41">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup> As his prayer was much integrated with traditional liturgy, Escrivá found the transition difficult and asked Echevarría to help him with respect to the new rites. Although he missed the practices of the old rites, especially some gestures such as the kissing of the <a href="/wiki/Paten" title="Paten">paten</a> (a small plate, usually made of silver or gold, used to hold Eucharistic bread), he prohibited his devotees to ask for any dispensation for him "out of a spirit of obedience to ecclesiastical norms... He has decided to show his love for the liturgy through the new rite", commented Echevarría. However, when <a href="/wiki/Annibale_Bugnini" title="Annibale Bugnini">Annibale Bugnini</a>, Secretary of the Consilium for the Implementation of the Constitution on the Liturgy, learned of Escrivá's difficulties, he granted Escrivá the possibility of celebrating the Mass using the old rite. Whenever Escrivá celebrated this rite, he did so only in the presence of one Mass server.<sup id="cite_ref-Echevarría_Rodríguez_2000_42-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Echevarría_Rodríguez_2000-42">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-43" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-43">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Vladimir Felzmann,<sup id="cite_ref-44" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-44">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup> a priest who worked as Escrivá's personal assistant before quitting Opus Dei in 1981,<sup id="cite_ref-Miller_45-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Miller-45">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup> claimed in an interview for <i><a href="/wiki/Newsweek" title="Newsweek">Newsweek</a></i> that Escrivá was so distraught by the reforms introduced by the Second Vatican Council that he and his deputy, <a href="/wiki/%C3%81lvaro_del_Portillo" title="Álvaro del Portillo">Álvaro del Portillo</a>, "went to Greece in 1967 to see if [they] could bring Opus Dei into the <a href="/wiki/Greek_Orthodox_Church" title="Greek Orthodox Church">Greek Orthodox Church</a>. Escrivá thought the [Catholic] church was a shambles and that the Orthodox might be the salvation of himself and of Opus Dei as the faithful remnant."<sup id="cite_ref-Woodward_46-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Woodward-46">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup>  Felzmann claims that Escrivá soon abandoned those plans as impracticable. Flavio Capucci, a member of Opus Dei and the postulator of the cause for Escrivá's <a href="/wiki/Canonization" title="Canonization">canonization</a>, denies that Escrivá ever contemplated quitting the Catholic Church.<sup id="cite_ref-Woodward_46-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Woodward-46">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup>  This was also denied by the information office of Opus Dei, which stated that Escrivá's visit to Greece in 1966 was done in order to analyze the convenience of organizing Opus Dei in that country, and that Escrivá even brought back <a href="/wiki/Icon" title="Icon">icons</a> as presents for Pope Paul VI and <a href="/wiki/Angelo_Dell%27Acqua" title="Angelo Dell&#39;Acqua">Angelo Dell'Acqua</a> (then the substitute to the <a href="/wiki/Secretariat_of_State_(Holy_See)" title="Secretariat of State (Holy See)">Vatican Secretary of State</a>), whom he had informed of the visit beforehand.<sup id="cite_ref-Felzmann-EP_47-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Felzmann-EP-47">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Mortification">Mortification</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Josemar%C3%ADa_Escriv%C3%A1&amp;action=edit&amp;section=10" title="Edit section: Mortification">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4><p>
Escrivá taught that "joy has its roots in the form of a cross", and that "suffering is the touchstone of love", convictions which were represented in his own life.<sup id="cite_ref-BIOG_48-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-BIOG-48">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup> He practiced <a href="/wiki/Mortification_of_the_flesh" title="Mortification of the flesh">corporal mortification</a> personally and recommended it to others in Opus Dei. In particular, his enthusiasm for the practice of self-<a href="/wiki/Flagellation" title="Flagellation">flagellation</a> has attracted controversy, with critics quoting testimonies about Escrivá whipping himself furiously until the walls of his cubicle were speckled with blood.<sup id="cite_ref-49" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-49">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup> Both the practice of self-mortification as a form of penance, and the conviction that suffering can help a person to acquire sanctity, have ample precedent in Catholic teaching and practice. Referring to Escrivá, John Paul II stated in <i>Christifideles omnes</i>:<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r996844942"/></p><blockquote class="templatequote"><p>During the Spanish Civil War he personally experienced the fury of anti-religious persecution and gave daily proof of heroism in a constant priestly activity seasoned with abundant prayer and penance. It did not take long before many came to consider him a saint. When the war was over many bishops invited him to preach retreats to their clergy, thereby greatly contributing to the renewal of Christian life in Spain. Many religious orders and congregations also requested his pastoral services. At the same time, God allowed him to suffer public attacks. He responded invariably with pardon, to the point of considering his detractors as benefactors. But this Cross was such a source of blessings from heaven that the Servant of God's apostolate spread with astonishing speed.<sup id="cite_ref-ACF_16-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ACF-16">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup></p></blockquote>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Towards_the_Virgin_Mary">Towards the Virgin Mary</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Josemar%C3%ADa_Escriv%C3%A1&amp;action=edit&amp;section=11" title="Edit section: Towards the Virgin Mary">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:202px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Univnavarrefairlove.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2c/Univnavarrefairlove.jpg/200px-Univnavarrefairlove.jpg" decoding="async" width="200" height="243" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2c/Univnavarrefairlove.jpg/300px-Univnavarrefairlove.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2c/Univnavarrefairlove.jpg/400px-Univnavarrefairlove.jpg 2x" data-file-width="633" data-file-height="768" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Univnavarrefairlove.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Mother of Fair Love, a gift of Josemaría Escrivá to the <a href="/wiki/University_of_Navarra" title="University of Navarra">University of Navarra</a>: John Paul II stated: "Love for our Lady is a constant characteristic of the life of Josemaría Escrivá."</div></div></div>
<p>Pope John Paul II stated on Sunday, 6 October 2002, after the Angelus greetings: "Love for our Lady is a constant characteristic of the life of Josemaría Escrivá and is an eminent part of the legacy that he left to his spiritual sons and daughters." The Pope also said that "St. Josemaría wrote a beautiful small book called <i>The Holy Rosary</i> which presents spiritual childhood, a real disposition of spirit of those who wish to attain total abandonment to the divine will".<sup id="cite_ref-50" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-50">&#91;50&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>When Escrivá was 10 or 11 years old, he already had the habit of carrying the rosary in his pocket. As a priest, he would ordinarily end his homilies and his personal prayer with a conversation with the Blessed Virgin. He instructed that all rooms in the offices of Opus Dei should have an image of the Virgin. He encouraged his spiritual children to greet these images when they entered a room. He encouraged a Marian apostolate, preaching that <i>"To Jesus we go and to Him we return through Mary"</i>. While looking at a picture of the <a href="/wiki/Virgin_of_Guadalupe" class="mw-redirect" title="Virgin of Guadalupe">Virgin of Guadalupe</a> giving a rose to San <a href="/wiki/Juan_Diego" title="Juan Diego">Juan Diego</a>, he commented: "I would like to die that way." On 26 June 1975, after entering his work room, which had a painting of the <a href="/wiki/Virgin_of_Guadalupe" class="mw-redirect" title="Virgin of Guadalupe">Virgin of Guadalupe</a>, he slumped on the floor and died.<sup id="cite_ref-BIOG_48-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-BIOG-48">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Towards_people">Towards people</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Josemar%C3%ADa_Escriv%C3%A1&amp;action=edit&amp;section=12" title="Edit section: Towards people">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>"Escrivá de Balaguer was a very human saint", preached John Paul II. "All those who met him, whatever their culture or social status, felt he was a father, totally devoted to serving others, for he was convinced that every soul is a marvellous treasure; indeed, every person is worth all of Christ's Blood. This attitude of service is obvious in his dedication to his priestly ministry and in the <a href="/wiki/Magnanimity" title="Magnanimity">magnanimity</a> with which he started so many works of evangelization and human advancement for the poorest persons."<sup id="cite_ref-JPII-2002_37-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-JPII-2002-37">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Former numerary María del Carmen Tapia (born 1925), who worked with Escrivá for 18 years inside the organization, seven as his secretary, wrote in her book, <i>Beyond the Threshold: A Life in Opus Dei</i>, that Escrivá often became angry, and that as secretary in charge of recording his words and actions, she was not allowed to record anything negative that she witnessed. She claims she was subjected to abusive words from Escrivá, who called her filthy names, and then screamed during this meeting with both men and women present, upbraiding a member who helped Tapia send letters. She was kept prisoner in the headquarters of Opus Dei in Rome from November 1965 until March 1966. "I was held completely deprived of any outside contact with the absolute prohibition to go out for any reason or receive or make telephone calls or to write or receive letters. Nor could I go out for the so-called weekly walk or the monthly excursion. I was a prisoner."<sup id="cite_ref-51" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-51">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>However, some of his devotees claim that, through him, Opus Dei has been able to better the quality of life of many women, and refer to his respect for women and his interest in improving their lives.<sup id="cite_ref-BIOG_48-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-BIOG-48">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup> Historian <a href="/wiki/Elizabeth_Fox-Genovese" title="Elizabeth Fox-Genovese">Elizabeth Fox-Genovese</a>, a Catholic convert, asserted that "Opus Dei has an enviable record of educating the poor and supporting women, whether single or married, in any occupation they choose."<sup id="cite_ref-BIOG_48-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-BIOG-48">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Towards_his_family">Towards his family</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Josemar%C3%ADa_Escriv%C3%A1&amp;action=edit&amp;section=13" title="Edit section: Towards his family">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Opus Dei's founder modified his name in several ways over the course of his life. In the church records of the cathedral at Barbastro, he appears as having been baptized four days after birth with the name José María Julián Mariano, and his surname was spelled Escriba.<sup id="cite_ref-Walsh-name_52-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Walsh-name-52">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup>  As early as his school days, José Escrivá had "adopted the rather more distinguished version spelled with a "v" rather than a "b."<sup id="cite_ref-53" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-53">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup> His name is spelled Escrivá in the memento of his first Mass. According to critics like Luis Carandell<sup id="cite_ref-Carandell-name_54-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Carandell-name-54">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup> and Michael Walsh<sup id="cite_ref-Walsh-name_52-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Walsh-name-52">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup> a former Jesuit priest, he also adopted the use of the conjunction <i>y</i> ("and") joining his father's and mother's surnames ("Escrivá y Albás"), a usage which he claims is associated with aristocratic families, even though that has been the legal naming format in Spain <a href="/wiki/Spanish_naming_customs#The_particle_.E2.80.9Cy.E2.80.9D_.28and.29" title="Spanish naming customs">since 1870</a>.
</p><p>On 16 June 1940, the Spanish <i><a href="/wiki/Bolet%C3%ADn_Oficial_del_Estado" title="Boletín Oficial del Estado">Boletín Oficial del Estado</a></i> ("Official State Bulletin") records that Escrivá requested of the government that he be permitted to change his "first surname so that it will be written Escrivá de Balaguer". He justified the petition on the grounds that "the name Escrivá is common in the east coast and in <a href="/wiki/Catalonia" title="Catalonia">Catalonia</a>, leading to harmful and annoying confusion".  On 20 June 1943, when he was 41 years old, the registry book of the Barbastro cathedral and the baptismal certificate of José María were annotated to represent "that the surname Escriba was changed to Escrivá de Balaguer".<sup id="cite_ref-Carandell-name_54-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Carandell-name-54">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup>  <a href="/wiki/Balaguer" title="Balaguer">Balaguer</a> is the name of the town in Catalonia from which Escrivá's paternal family derived.
</p><p>One of the earliest members of Opus Dei, and a friend for many years, the architect Miguel Fisac, who later quit Opus Dei, said that Escrivá found it embarrassing to have his father's family name since his father's company became bankrupt, that he had a "great affection for the aristocracy", and that, when Escrivá was a chaplain at the Foundation of Santa Isabel in Madrid, he would often meet aristocratic visitors who would ask, upon learning that his name was Escrivá, whether he belonged to the noble Escrivá de Romaní family, only to ignore him when they learned that he did not.<sup id="cite_ref-Fisac1_29-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Fisac1-29">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup> According to Vásquez de Prada, a writer, Opus Dei member, and official biographer who produced a three-volume biography of Escrivá, the act had nothing to do with ambition but was motivated rather by fairness and loyalty to his family.<sup id="cite_ref-VDP_12-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-VDP-12">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup> The main problem is that in Spanish the letters <i>b</i> and <i>v</i> are pronounced in the same way and therefore bureaucrats and clerics had made mistakes in transcribing the Escrivá family name in some official documents throughout the generations. Defenders of Escrivá have also argued that the addition of "de Balaguer" corresponded to a practice adopted by many Spanish families that felt a need to distinguish themselves from others with the same surname but proceeding from <a href="/wiki/Spanish_naming_customs#The_particle_.E2.80.9Cde.E2.80.9D_.28of.29" title="Spanish naming customs">different regions</a> and consequently having different histories.
</p><p>Escrivá's younger brother Santiago stated that his brother "loved the members of his family" and took good care of them.<sup id="cite_ref-SANTIAGO_55-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-SANTIAGO-55">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup> When their father died, he says, Escrivá told their mother that "she should stay calm, because he will always take care of us. And he fulfilled this promise." Escrivá would find time in his busy schedule to chat and take a walk with his younger brother, acting like a father towards him. When the family transferred to Madrid, he obeyed the instructions of their father that he obtain a doctorate in Law. "Thanks to his docility to this advice", says Santiago, "he was able to support the family by giving classes in Law, and with this he acquired a juridical mentality ... which would later be so necessary to do Opus Dei." Escrivá also modified his first name. From José María, he changed it to the original Josemaría. Biographers state, that around 1935 [age 33], "he joined his first two names because his single love for the Virgin Mary and Saint Joseph were equally inseparable".<sup id="cite_ref-VDP_12-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-VDP-12">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Towards_his_country">Towards his country</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Josemar%C3%ADa_Escriv%C3%A1&amp;action=edit&amp;section=14" title="Edit section: Towards his country">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Many of his contemporaries recount the tendency of Escrivá to preach about patriotism as opposed to nationalism.<sup id="cite_ref-56" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-56">&#91;56&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-57" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-57">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Critics have alleged that Escrivá personally, as well as the organization of Opus Dei, were associated originally with the ideology of "National Catholicism", particularly during the <a href="/wiki/Spanish_Civil_War" title="Spanish Civil War">Spanish Civil War</a> and the years immediately after it, and that they were therefore also closely associated with the authoritarian regime of <a href="/wiki/Francisco_Franco" title="Francisco Franco">General Franco</a>. According to <a href="/wiki/Catalonia" title="Catalonia">Catalan</a> sociologist Joan Estruch:
</p>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r996844942"/><blockquote class="templatequote"><p>More than "a classic of the spirituality of all time", Escrivá de Balaguer is at bottom <i>a child of his time</i>: he is the product of a specific country, a specific epoch, a specific church. These are the <a href="/wiki/Francoist_Spain" title="Francoist Spain">Spain of General Franco</a> and the church of <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_X" title="Pope Pius X">Pope Pius X</a>. If Opus Dei had "never seen the need to bring itself up to date", as Escrivá maintained, Opus would today be a <a href="/wiki/Paramilitary" title="Paramilitary">paramilitary</a>, pro-<a href="/wiki/Fascism" title="Fascism">fascist</a>, <a href="/wiki/Modernism_(Roman_Catholicism)" class="mw-redirect" title="Modernism (Roman Catholicism)">antimodernist</a>, <a href="/wiki/Integrism" class="mw-redirect" title="Integrism">integralist</a> (reactionary) organization.  If it is not, it is because it has evolved over time, just as the Catholic Church, the Franco regime, and Msgr. Escrivá himself evolved.<sup id="cite_ref-58" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-58">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup></p></blockquote>
<p>Estruch cites, for instance, the fact that the first edition of Escrivá's <i>The Way</i>, finished in <a href="/wiki/Burgos" title="Burgos">Burgos</a> and published in <a href="/wiki/Valencia,_Spain" class="mw-redirect" title="Valencia, Spain">Valencia</a> in 1939, had the dateline <i>Año de la Victoria</i> ("Year of the Victory"), referring to Franco's military triumph over the <a href="/wiki/Republican_Faction_(Spanish_Civil_War)" class="mw-redirect" title="Republican Faction (Spanish Civil War)">Republican</a> forces in the civil war,<sup id="cite_ref-59" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-59">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup> as well as a prologue by a pro-Franco bishop, Xavier Lauzurica, which ended with the admonition to the reader to "always stay vigilant and alert, because the enemy does not sleep. If you make these maxims your life, you will be a perfect imitator of Jesus Christ and a gentleman without blemish. And with Christs like you Spain will return to the old grandeur of its saints, wise men, and heroes."<sup id="cite_ref-60" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-60">&#91;60&#93;</a></sup> Escrivá preached personally to General Franco and his family during a week-long spiritual retreat at the <a href="/wiki/Royal_Palace_of_El_Pardo" title="Royal Palace of El Pardo">Pardo Palace</a> (Franco's official residence) in April 1946.<sup id="cite_ref-61" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-61">&#91;61&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Vittorio_Messori" title="Vittorio Messori">Vittorio Messori</a> claims that the ties between Escrivá and Francoism are part of a <a href="/wiki/Black_legend" title="Black legend">black legend</a> propagated against Escrivá and Opus Dei.<sup id="cite_ref-MESSORI_62-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-MESSORI-62">&#91;62&#93;</a></sup> Allen states that based on his research Escrivá could not be said to be <i>pro-Franco</i> (for which he was criticized for not joining other Catholics in openly praising Franco) nor <i>anti-Franco</i> (for which he was criticized for not being "pro-democracy"). According to Allen, there is no statement from Escrivá for or against Franco.<sup id="cite_ref-ALLENBOOOK_10-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ALLENBOOOK-10">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup> Escrivá's devotees and some historians have emphasized his personal effort to avoid partiality in politics. Professor <a href="/wiki/Peter_Berglar" title="Peter Berglar">Peter Berglar</a>, a German historian, asserts that Franco's <a href="/wiki/Falangists" class="mw-redirect" title="Falangists">falangists</a> suspected Escrivá of "internationalism, anti-Spainism and Freemasonry" and that during "the first decade of Franco's regime, Opus Dei and Escrivá were attacked with perseverance bordering on fanaticism, not by enemies, but by supporters of the new <a href="/wiki/Spanish_State" class="mw-redirect" title="Spanish State">Spanish State</a>. Escrivá was even reported to the <i>Tribunal for the Fight against Freemasonry</i>".<sup id="cite_ref-63" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-63">&#91;63&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Awards_and_honors">Awards and honors</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Josemar%C3%ADa_Escriv%C3%A1&amp;action=edit&amp;section=15" title="Edit section: Awards and honors">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Escrivá received several awards:
</p>
<ul><li>The Grand Cross of Alfonso X the Wise (1951)</li>
<li>The Gold Cross of St. Raymond of Penyafort (1954)</li>
<li>The <a href="/wiki/Order_of_Isabella_the_Catholic" title="Order of Isabella the Catholic">Grand Cross of Isabella the Catholic</a> (1956)</li>
<li>The <a href="/wiki/Order_of_Charles_III" title="Order of Charles III">Grand Cross of Charles III</a> (1960)</li>
<li>Doctor Honoris Causa by the University of Zaragoza (Spain, 1960)</li>
<li>The Gold Medal by the City council of <a href="/wiki/Barbastro" title="Barbastro">Barbastro</a> (1975).</li></ul>
<p>Some biographers have said that Escrivá did not seek these awards, that they were nevertheless granted to him, that he accepted them due to charity to those who were granting these, and that he did not give the slightest importance to these awards.<sup id="cite_ref-BIOG_48-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-BIOG-48">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup>  Journalist Luis Carandell, however, recounts testimonies about how members of Opus Dei paid for the insignia of the Grand Cross of Charles III to be made from gold, only to have Escrivá angrily reject it and demand instead one encrusted with <a href="/wiki/Diamond_(gemstone)" title="Diamond (gemstone)">diamonds</a>. Carandell holds that this episode was part of a larger pattern in Escrivá's life of ambition for social prestige and the trappings of wealth.<sup id="cite_ref-64" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-64">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup> Sympathetic biographers, however, insist that Escrivá taught that material things are good, but that people should not get attached to them and should serve only God.  It is reported that he declared that "he has most who needs least" and that it took only 10 minutes to gather his possessions after his death.<sup id="cite_ref-BIOG_48-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-BIOG-48">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Controversies">Controversies</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Josemar%C3%ADa_Escriv%C3%A1&amp;action=edit&amp;section=16" title="Edit section: Controversies">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>In addition to the questions raised about the depth of Escrivá's spirituality and theological thinking, his purported habits of secretiveness and elitism (although for the most part, Opus Dei faithful belong to the middle-to-low levels of society, in terms of education, income and social status),<sup id="cite_ref-MESSORI_62-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-MESSORI-62">&#91;62&#93;</a></sup> his alleged bad temper and ambition for social status and worldly luxuries, several other specific aspects of Escrivá's life and work have generated some criticism, particularly regarding his <a href="/wiki/Canonization" title="Canonization">canonization</a> by the Catholic Church. The sources of criticism include his alleged private statements in defence of <a href="/wiki/Adolf_Hitler" title="Adolf Hitler">Adolf Hitler</a>, collaboration by members of Opus Dei with right-wing political causes (especially during General <a href="/wiki/Francisco_Franco" title="Francisco Franco">Francisco Franco</a>'s dictatorship in <a href="/wiki/Spain" title="Spain">Spain</a>), Escrivá's request for the rehabilitation in his favour of an <a href="/wiki/Nobility" title="Nobility">aristocratic</a> title and allegations that he maintained bad relations with other Catholic officials, of whom he could be very critical in private.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Alleged_defence_of_Hitler">Alleged defence of Hitler</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Josemar%C3%ADa_Escriv%C3%A1&amp;action=edit&amp;section=17" title="Edit section: Alleged defence of Hitler">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>During Escrivá's beatification process, Vladimir Felzmann, who had been Escrivá's personal assistant before Felzmann quit Opus Dei and became a priest of the <a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Archdiocese_of_Westminster" class="mw-redirect" title="Roman Catholic Archdiocese of Westminster">Roman Catholic Archdiocese of Westminster</a> and an aide to <a href="/wiki/Basil_Hume" title="Basil Hume">Cardinal Basil Hume</a>, sent several letters to Flavio Capucci, the postulator (i.e., chief promoter) of Escrivá's cause. In his letters, Felzmann claimed that in 1967 or 1968, during the intermission to a <a href="/wiki/World_War_II" title="World War II">World War II</a> movie, Escrivá had said to him, "Vlad, Hitler couldn't have been such a bad person. He couldn't have killed six million. It couldn't have been more than four million".<sup id="cite_ref-Woodward_46-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Woodward-46">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Thompson_65-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Thompson-65">&#91;65&#93;</a></sup> Felzmann later explained that those remarks should be regarded in the context of Catholic <a href="/wiki/Anticommunism" class="mw-redirect" title="Anticommunism">anticommunism</a> in Spain, emphasizing that in 1941 all of the male members of Opus Dei, who then numbered about fifty, offered to join the "<a href="/wiki/Blue_Division" title="Blue Division">Blue Division</a>", a group of Spaniard volunteers who joined the <a href="/wiki/Wehrmacht" title="Wehrmacht">German forces</a> in their fight against the <a href="/wiki/Soviet_Army" title="Soviet Army">Soviet Army</a>, along the <a href="/wiki/Eastern_Front_(World_War_II)" title="Eastern Front (World War II)">Eastern Front</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Felzmann-EP_47-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Felzmann-EP-47">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-66" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-66">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup> Another phrase that has been attributed to Escrivá by some of his critics is "Hitler against the Jews, Hitler against the Slavs, means Hitler against Communism".<sup id="cite_ref-67" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-67">&#91;67&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/%C3%81lvaro_del_Portillo" title="Álvaro del Portillo">Álvaro del Portillo</a>, who succeeded Escrivá as the director of Opus Dei, declared that any claims that Escrivá endorsed Hitler were "a patent falsehood" and part of "a slanderous campaign".<sup id="cite_ref-del_Portillo_1996_27-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-del_Portillo_1996-27">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup> He and others have stated that Escrivá regarded Hitler as a "pagan", a "racist" and a "tyrant"<sup id="cite_ref-68" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-68">&#91;68&#93;</a></sup> (see <a href="/wiki/Opus_Dei_and_politics" title="Opus Dei and politics">Opus Dei and politics</a>).
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Alleged_support_for_right-wing_leaders">Alleged support for right-wing leaders</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Josemar%C3%ADa_Escriv%C3%A1&amp;action=edit&amp;section=18" title="Edit section: Alleged support for right-wing leaders">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>One of the most controversial accusations against Escrivá is that he and Opus Dei were active in bolstering far-right regimes,<sup id="cite_ref-BBCDECODING_69-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-BBCDECODING-69">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup> especially the dictatorship of General <a href="/wiki/Francisco_Franco" title="Francisco Franco">Francisco Franco</a> in Spain.  After 1957, several members of Opus Dei served as ministers in Franco's government.<sup id="cite_ref-70" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-70">&#91;70&#93;</a></sup> In particular, the "technocrats" most associated with the "<a href="/wiki/Spanish_miracle" title="Spanish miracle">Spanish miracle</a>" of the 1960s were members of Opus Dei: <a href="/wiki/Alberto_Ullastres" title="Alberto Ullastres">Alberto Ullastres</a>, Mariano Navarro Rubio, <a href="/wiki/Gregorio_L%C3%B3pez-Bravo_y_Castro" class="mw-redirect" title="Gregorio López-Bravo y Castro">Gregorio López-Bravo</a>, <a href="/wiki/Laureano_L%C3%B3pez_Rod%C3%B3" title="Laureano López Rodó">Laureano López Rodó</a>, Juan José Espinosa, and Faustino García-Moncó.  Most of these "technocrats" entered the government under the patronage of Admiral <a href="/wiki/Luis_Carrero_Blanco" title="Luis Carrero Blanco">Luis Carrero Blanco</a> who, though not a member of Opus Dei himself, was reportedly sympathetic to the organization and its values and who, as Franco grew older and more frail, increasingly came to exercise the day-to-day control of the Spanish government.<sup id="cite_ref-71" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-71">&#91;71&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>According to journalist Luis Carandell, when Ullastres and Navarro Rubio were first appointed to the government in 1957, Escrivá gleefully exclaimed "They have made us ministers!"<sup id="cite_ref-72" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-72">&#91;72&#93;</a></sup> something which Opus Dei has officially denied.<sup id="cite_ref-73" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-73">&#91;73&#93;</a></sup>  On 23 May 1958, Escrivá sent a letter to Franco, which said, in part:
</p>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r996844942"/><blockquote class="templatequote"><p>Although a stranger to any political activity, I cannot help but rejoice as a priest and Spaniard that the Chief of State’s authoritative voice should proclaim that, "The Spanish nation considers it a badge of honour to accept the law of God according to the one and true doctrine of the Holy Catholic Church, inseparable faith of the national conscience which will inspire its legislation." It is in fidelity to our people’s Catholic tradition that the best guarantee of success in acts of government, the certainty of a just and lasting peace within the national community, as well as the divine blessing for those holding positions of authority, will always be found. I ask God our Lord to bestow upon your Excellency with every sort felicity and impart abundant grace to carry out the grave mission entrusted to you.<sup id="cite_ref-74" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-74">&#91;74&#93;</a></sup></p></blockquote>
<p>In 1963, Swiss Catholic theologian <a href="/wiki/Hans_Urs_von_Balthasar" title="Hans Urs von Balthasar">Hans Urs von Balthasar</a> wrote a scathing critique of Escrivá's spirituality, characterizing Escrivá's approach religion as a form of "<a href="/wiki/Integrism" class="mw-redirect" title="Integrism">integrism</a>", stating "despite the affirmations of the members of Opus Dei that they are free in their political options, it is undeniable that its foundation is marked by <a href="/wiki/Francoism" class="mw-redirect" title="Francoism">Francoism</a>, that that is the 'law within which it has been formed'".<sup id="cite_ref-Balthasar_38-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Balthasar-38">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup> In another essay, published the following year, von Balthasar characterized Opus Dei as "an integrist concentration of power within the Church", explaining that the main objective of integrism is "imposing the spiritual with worldly means".<sup id="cite_ref-Balthasar-2_75-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Balthasar-2-75">&#91;75&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1979, von Balthasar distanced himself from a newspaper attack on Opus Dei which had cited his earlier accusations of integrism.  He wrote in a personal letter to the Prelature, sent also to the <i>Neue Zürcher Zeitung</i>, that "because of my lack of concrete information, I am not able to give an informed opinion about Opus Dei today. On the other hand, one thing strikes me as obvious: many of the criticisms levelled against the movement, including those of your own journal concerning the religious instruction given by Opus Dei members, seem to me to be false and anti-clerical."<sup id="cite_ref-MESSORI_62-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-MESSORI-62">&#91;62&#93;</a></sup>  Von Balthasar maintained his unfavourable judgment of Escrivá's spirituality and repeated it in a television interview in 1984, but he did not renew his criticism of Opus Dei as an organization.<sup id="cite_ref-Allen_2005_64_39-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Allen_2005_64-39">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup>  In 1988, von Balthasar was named as a <a href="/wiki/Cardinal_(Catholic_Church)" title="Cardinal (Catholic Church)">cardinal</a> by <a href="/wiki/Pope_John_Paul_II" title="Pope John Paul II">Pope John Paul II</a>, but he died before he could be elevated to that position at the next <a href="/wiki/Papal_consistory" title="Papal consistory">consistory</a>.
</p><p>In response to the accusations of "integrism", Escrivá declared that, "Opus Dei is neither on the left nor on the right nor in the centre" and that "as regards religious liberty, from its foundation Opus Dei has never practised discrimination of any kind."<sup id="cite_ref-76" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-76">&#91;76&#93;</a></sup>  Opus Dei officials state that individual members are free to choose any political affiliation, emphasizing that among its members were also two important figures in the <a href="/wiki/Monarchy_of_Spain" title="Monarchy of Spain">monarchist</a> political opposition of the 1970s in Spain: the writer <a href="/wiki/Rafael_Calvo_Serer" title="Rafael Calvo Serer">Rafael Calvo Serer</a>, who was forced into exile by Franco's regime, and the journalist <a href="/wiki/Antonio_Font%C3%A1n" title="Antonio Fontán">Antonio Fontán</a>, who became the first president of the Senate after the transition to democracy.
</p><p>John Allen has written that Escrivá was neither anti-Franco nor pro-Franco.<sup id="cite_ref-Allen_2005_61_77-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Allen_2005_61-77">&#91;77&#93;</a></sup>  Some critics of Opus Dei, such as Miguel Fisac<sup id="cite_ref-Fisac1_29-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Fisac1-29">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup> and <a href="/wiki/Damian_Thompson" title="Damian Thompson">Damian Thompson</a>, have argued that the group has always sought "advancement not only of its message but also of its interests",<sup id="cite_ref-Thompson-Allen_78-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Thompson-Allen-78">&#91;78&#93;</a></sup> and that it has consistently courted those with power and influence, without maintaining a coherent political ideology.
</p><p>The alleged involvement of Opus Dei in Latin American politics has also been a topic of controversy.  According to US journalist <a href="/wiki/Penny_Lernoux" title="Penny Lernoux">Penny Lernoux</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Argentine_Revolution" title="Argentine Revolution">1966 military coup</a> in Argentina happened soon after its leader, General <a href="/wiki/Juan_Carlos_Ongan%C3%ADa" title="Juan Carlos Onganía">Juan Carlos Onganía</a>, attended a spiritual retreat sponsored by Opus Dei.<sup id="cite_ref-Walsh_2004_131–132_79-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Walsh_2004_131–132-79">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup>  During his 1974 visit to Latin America, Escrivá visited <a href="/wiki/Chile" title="Chile">Chile</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-80" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-80">&#91;80&#93;</a></sup> only nine months after the <a href="/wiki/1973_Chilean_coup_d%27%C3%A9tat" title="1973 Chilean coup d&#39;état">coup d'état</a> in Chile that deposed the elected <a href="/wiki/Marxism" title="Marxism">Marxist</a> president <a href="/wiki/Salvador_Allende" title="Salvador Allende">Salvador Allende</a> and installed a right-wing military dictatorship under General <a href="/wiki/Augusto_Pinochet" title="Augusto Pinochet">Augusto Pinochet</a>.  Escrivá declined an invitation to visit personally with the Chilean <a href="/wiki/Government_Junta_of_Chile_(1973)" title="Government Junta of Chile (1973)">government junta</a>, alleging that he was ill with <a href="/wiki/Influenza" title="Influenza">influenza</a>, but in his letter to the members of the junta he added that he wished "to let you know how much I pray, have prayed, and have gotten others to pray, for this great nation, especially when it found itself menaced by the scourge of the Marxist heresy."<sup id="cite_ref-81" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-81">&#91;81&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Critics have charged that Opus Dei members supported Pinochet's coup and then had a role in the "<a href="/wiki/Miracle_of_Chile" title="Miracle of Chile">Miracle of Chile</a>" of the 1980s similar to that of the "technocrats" during the Spanish Miracle of the 1960s.<sup id="cite_ref-Walsh_2004_131–132_79-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Walsh_2004_131–132-79">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-82" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-82">&#91;82&#93;</a></sup>  However, among the major right-wing politicians, only <a href="/wiki/Joaqu%C3%ADn_Lav%C3%ADn" title="Joaquín Lavín">Joaquín Lavín</a> (who did not occupy public office under Pinochet) has been unequivocally identified as a member of Opus Dei.<sup id="cite_ref-83" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-83">&#91;83&#93;</a></sup>  Another member of Opus Dei, Jorge Sabag Villalobos, belongs to a centre-left party that opposed Pinochet's regime.<sup id="cite_ref-84" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-84">&#91;84&#93;</a></sup>  <a href="/wiki/Peter_Berglar" title="Peter Berglar">Peter Berglar</a>, a German historian and member of Opus Dei, has written that connecting Opus Dei with fascist regimes is a "gross slander".<sup id="cite_ref-85" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-85">&#91;85&#93;</a></sup> Journalist <a href="/wiki/Noam_Friedlander" title="Noam Friedlander">Noam Friedlander</a> states that allegations about Opus Dei involvement with the Pinochet regime are "unproven tales."<sup id="cite_ref-Noam_6-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Noam-6">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup> Several of Escrivá's collaborators stated that he actually despised dictatorships.<sup id="cite_ref-del_Portillo_1996_27-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-del_Portillo_1996-27">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Echevarría_Rodríguez_2000_42-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Echevarría_Rodríguez_2000-42">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-86" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-86">&#91;86&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Title_of_nobility">Title of nobility</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Josemar%C3%ADa_Escriv%C3%A1&amp;action=edit&amp;section=19" title="Edit section: Title of nobility">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Another source of controversy about Escrivá was the fact that, in 1968, he requested and received from the Spanish <a href="/wiki/Ministry_of_the_Interior_(Spain)" title="Ministry of the Interior (Spain)">Ministry of Justice</a> the rehabilitation in his favor of the <a href="/wiki/Nobility" title="Nobility">aristocratic</a> title of <a href="/wiki/Marquess" title="Marquess">Marquess</a> of <a href="/wiki/Peralta_de_Calasanz" title="Peralta de Calasanz">Peralta</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-delaCierva_87-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-delaCierva-87">&#91;87&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-88" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-88">&#91;88&#93;</a></sup> According to the official <i>Guía de grandezas y títulos del reino</i> ("Guide to the <a href="/wiki/Grandee" title="Grandee">grandeeships</a> and titles of the realm"), the title of Marquess had originally been granted in 1718 to Tomás de Peralta, minister of state, justice and war for the <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Naples" title="Kingdom of Naples">Kingdom of Naples</a>, by <a href="/wiki/Charles_VI,_Holy_Roman_Emperor" title="Charles VI, Holy Roman Emperor">Archduke Charles of Austria</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-89" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-89">&#91;89&#93;</a></sup> Until 1715, Archduke Charles had been, as "Charles III", a pretender to the Spanish throne (<i>see</i> <a href="/wiki/War_of_Spanish_Succession" class="mw-redirect" title="War of Spanish Succession">War of Spanish Succession</a>), and from 1711 until 1740 he ruled as <a href="/wiki/Holy_Roman_Emperor" title="Holy Roman Emperor">Holy Roman Emperor</a> and <a href="/wiki/King_of_Naples" class="mw-redirect" title="King of Naples">King of Naples</a>.
</p><p>Escrivá's successful petition of a title of nobility has aroused controversy not only because it might seem at odds with the humility befitting a Catholic priest, but also because the same title of Marquess of Peralta had been rehabilitated in 1883 by <a href="/wiki/Pope_Leo_XIII" title="Pope Leo XIII">Pope Leo XIII</a> and King <a href="/wiki/Alfonso_XII_of_Spain" class="mw-redirect" title="Alfonso XII of Spain">Alfonso XII</a> in favor of a man to whom Escrivá had no male-line family association: the <a href="/wiki/Costa_Rica" title="Costa Rica">Costa Rican</a> diplomat <a href="/wiki/Manuel_Mar%C3%ADa_de_Peralta_y_Alfaro" title="Manuel María de Peralta y Alfaro">Manuel María de Peralta y Alfaro</a> (1847–1930).<sup id="cite_ref-Borel_90-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Borel-90">&#91;90&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Saenz_91-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Saenz-91">&#91;91&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-92" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-92">&#91;92&#93;</a></sup> On that occasion, the documents ordering the rehabilitation claimed that the original title had been granted in 1738 (not 1718) to Juan Tomás de Peralta y Franco de Medina, by Charles of Austria in his capacity as Holy Roman Emperor, not as pretender to the Spanish throne. Ambassador Peralta, who in 1884 had married a <a href="/wiki/Belgium" title="Belgium">Belgian</a> <a href="/wiki/Count" title="Count">countess</a>, Jehanne de Clérembault,<sup id="cite_ref-Borel_90-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Borel-90">&#91;90&#93;</a></sup> died without children in 1930.<sup id="cite_ref-Saenz_91-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Saenz-91">&#91;91&#93;</a></sup> None of his kinsmen in Costa Rica requested the transmission of the marquessate, but one of them has published an extensive genealogical study that would seem to contradict any claim by Escrivá to the title.<sup id="cite_ref-delaCierva_87-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-delaCierva-87">&#91;87&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-93" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-93">&#91;93&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-94" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-94">&#91;94&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Escrivá did not use the title of Marquess of Peralta publicly and, in 1972, he ceded it to his brother Santiago.  The argument by endorsers of Escrivá that he requested the rehabilitation of the title as a favor to his family, and that it was his intention from the beginning to cede it to his brother, seems belied by the fact that, in 1968, Santiago had requested for himself the rehabilitation of a different title of nobility, the <a href="/wiki/Baron" title="Baron">barony</a> of San Felipe, which was not granted.<sup id="cite_ref-95" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-95">&#91;95&#93;</a></sup> According to historian <a href="/wiki/Ricardo_de_la_Cierva" title="Ricardo de la Cierva">Ricardo de la Cierva</a> (a former <a href="/wiki/Ministry_of_Culture_(Spain)" title="Ministry of Culture (Spain)">Minister of Culture</a> in the Spanish government) and to architect Miguel Fisac (who knew Escrivá personally at the time), Escrivá's original request for the title might have been part of an unsuccessful attempt to join the <a href="/wiki/Sovereign_Military_Order_of_Malta" title="Sovereign Military Order of Malta">Sovereign Military Order of Malta</a> (SMOM), a Catholic religious order which required its members to be of noble birth and of which his deputy in Opus Dei, <a href="/wiki/%C3%81lvaro_del_Portillo" title="Álvaro del Portillo">Álvaro del Portillo</a>, was already a member.<sup id="cite_ref-Fisac1_29-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Fisac1-29">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-delaCierva_87-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-delaCierva-87">&#91;87&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Several biographers say Escrivá prohibited his devotees from asking for the title of Marquess of Peralta. They state that Escrivá accepted it due to the advice of some cardinals who told him that he had the obligation to do so for the sake of his brother, Santiago, and so as to practice what he preached about fulfilling civil duties and exercising rights.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2012)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> His brother Santiago said: "The decision was heroic because he knew that he will be vilified as a result... Josemaría did what is best for me. After the right amount of time has passed, without making use of the title (in fact he never had the intention of using it), he passed the title on to me."<sup id="cite_ref-SANTIAGO_55-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-SANTIAGO-55">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup>  On the other hand, according to de la Cierva, "Monsignor Escrivá's desire for a marquisate is not to my liking but seems to me, given his idiosyncrasies, comprehensible and even forgivable.  That the title should have been based on a falsification seems to me extremely sad and even extremely grave."<sup id="cite_ref-delaCierva_87-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-delaCierva-87">&#91;87&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Relations_with_other_Catholic_leaders">Relations with other Catholic leaders</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Josemar%C3%ADa_Escriv%C3%A1&amp;action=edit&amp;section=20" title="Edit section: Relations with other Catholic leaders">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
