<p><a href="/wiki/Society_of_St._Paul" class="mw-redirect" title="Society of St. Paul">Pauline priest</a> Giancarlo Rocca, a church historian and a professor at the <i><a href="/wiki/Institute_of_the_Theology_of_the_Consecrated_Life_Claretianum" class="mw-redirect" title="Institute of the Theology of the Consecrated Life Claretianum">Claretianum</a></i> in Rome, claims that Escrivá actively sought the rank of <a href="/wiki/Bishop_(Catholic_Church)" class="mw-redirect" title="Bishop (Catholic Church)">bishop</a> but was twice refused by the <a href="/wiki/Roman_Curia" title="Roman Curia">Vatican curia</a>, first in 1945, and later in 1950 (when he and his followers had lobbied for his appointment as <a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Diocese_of_Vitoria" title="Roman Catholic Diocese of Vitoria">bishop of Vitoria</a>). According to Rocca, in both instances the curial officials expressed concerns about the organization of Opus Dei and about the psychological profile of Escrivá.<sup id="cite_ref-Rocca_18-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Rocca-18">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Sociologist Alberto Moncada, a former member of Opus Dei, has collected and published various oral testimonies about Escrivá's strained relations with other officials of the Catholic Church.<sup id="cite_ref-Moncada-oral_96-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Moncada-oral-96">&#91;96&#93;</a></sup> In particular, Moncada quotes Antonio Pérez-Tenessa, who at the time was secretary general of Opus Dei in Rome, as witnessing Escrivá's intense displeasure over the election of <a href="/wiki/Pope_Paul_VI" title="Pope Paul VI">Pope Paul VI</a> in 1963, and later expressing doubts in private about the salvation of the Pope's soul.<sup id="cite_ref-Moncada-oral_96-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Moncada-oral-96">&#91;96&#93;</a></sup>  According to María del Carmen Tapia, who worked with Escrivá in Rome, the founder of Opus Dei had "no respect" for Popes <a href="/wiki/Pope_John_XXIII" title="Pope John XXIII">John XXIII</a> or Paul VI and believed that his own organization of Opus Dei was "above the Church in holiness."<sup id="cite_ref-Woodward_46-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Woodward-46">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Luigi_de_Magistris_(cardinal)" class="mw-redirect" title="Luigi de Magistris (cardinal)">Luigi de Magistris</a>, at the time the regent of the Vatican's <a href="/wiki/Apostolic_Penitentiary" title="Apostolic Penitentiary">Apostolic Penitentiary</a>, wrote in a 1989 confidential vote asking for the suspension of the proceedings for Escrivá's beatification, that "it is not a mystery that there were serious tensions" between Escrivá and the <a href="/wiki/Society_of_Jesus" class="mw-redirect" title="Society of Jesus">Jesuits</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-VoteSuspension_97-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-VoteSuspension-97">&#91;97&#93;</a></sup>  De Magistris then alluded to Escrivá distancing himself from the Jesuit priest Valentino Sánchez, who had previously been Escrivá's confessor, over the Jesuits' opposition to the proposed constitutions of Opus Dei.<sup id="cite_ref-VoteSuspension_97-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-VoteSuspension-97">&#91;97&#93;</a></sup>  Journalist Luis Carandell claims that, during his years in Rome, Escrivá kept his distance from the Jesuit <a href="/wiki/Superior_General_of_the_Society_of_Jesus" title="Superior General of the Society of Jesus">Superior General</a>, the Spaniard <a href="/wiki/Pedro_Arrupe" title="Pedro Arrupe">Pedro Arrupe</a>, to the extent that Arrupe once joked with <a href="/wiki/Antonio_Riberi" title="Antonio Riberi">Antonio Riberi</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Nuncio" title="Nuncio">apostolic nuncio</a> to Spain, about doubting whether Escrivá really existed.<sup id="cite_ref-98" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-98">&#91;98&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>According to Alberto Moncada, Escrivá's years in Rome were dedicated in large part to his campaign to make Opus Dei independent from the authority of <a href="/wiki/Catholic_Church_hierarchy#Diocesan_bishops" class="mw-redirect" title="Catholic Church hierarchy">diocesan bishops</a> and of the Vatican curia, something which was finally achieved, after Escrivá's death, with the establishment in 1982, by Pope <a href="/wiki/John_Paul_II" class="mw-redirect" title="John Paul II">John Paul II</a>, of Opus Dei as a <a href="/wiki/Personal_prelature" title="Personal prelature">personal prelature</a>, subject only to its own prelate and to the Pope.<sup id="cite_ref-Moncada-oral_96-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Moncada-oral-96">&#91;96&#93;</a></sup> As such, Opus Dei is currently the only personal prelature in the Catholic Church, although this juridical figure ―similar in nature to other kinds of hierarchical organization in the church's history, such as military and personal ordinariates― is fruit of the Second Vatican Council's aim to provide pastoral attention in ways more suited to the actual situation of many of its faithful.<sup id="cite_ref-Prelatures,_Ordinariates_99-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Prelatures,_Ordinariates-99">&#91;99&#93;</a></sup> In this way, its work complements that of the dioceses, and in some cases even takes the form of a more direct collaboration: for example, when priests of Opus Dei assume pastoral care of parishes at the request of the local bishops.<sup id="cite_ref-Dolan_100-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Dolan-100">&#91;100&#93;</a></sup> Escrivá may have had this in mind when he wrote, "The only ambition, the only desire of Opus Dei and each of its members is to serve the Church as the Church wants to be served, within the specific vocation God has given us."<sup id="cite_ref-101" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-101">&#91;101&#93;</a></sup> Membership in the prelature does not exempt a Catholic from the authority of the local diocesan bishop.<sup id="cite_ref-ALLENBOOOK_10-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ALLENBOOOK-10">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Beatification_and_canonization">Beatification and canonization</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Josemar%C3%ADa_Escriv%C3%A1&amp;action=edit&amp;section=21" title="Edit section: Beatification and canonization">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Canonization_of_Josemar%C3%ADa_Escriv%C3%A1" title="Canonization of Josemaría Escrivá">Canonization of Josemaría Escrivá</a></div>
<p>After the death of Escrivá de Balaguer on 26 June 1975, the Postulation for the Cause of his beatification and canonization received many testimonies and postulatory letters from people all over the world. On the fifth anniversary of Escrivá's death, the Postulation solicited the initiation of the cause of <a href="/wiki/Beatification" title="Beatification">beatification</a> from the Vatican <a href="/wiki/Congregation_for_the_Causes_of_Saints" class="mw-redirect" title="Congregation for the Causes of Saints">Congregation for the Causes of Saints</a>. One-third of the world's bishops (an unprecedented number) petitioned for Escrivá's beatification.<sup id="cite_ref-MESSORI_62-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-MESSORI-62">&#91;62&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>His cause for beatification was introduced in Rome on 19 February 1981 on the strength of the apparently <a href="/wiki/Miracle" title="Miracle">miraculous</a> cure in 1976 of a rare <a href="/wiki/Disease" title="Disease">disease</a>, <a href="/wiki/Lipomatosis" title="Lipomatosis">lipomatosis</a>, suffered by Sister Concepción Boullón Rubio, whose family had prayed to Escrivá to help her. On 9 April 1990, <a href="/wiki/Pope_John_Paul_II" title="Pope John Paul II">Pope John Paul II</a> declared that Escrivá possessed Christian virtues to a "heroic degree", and on 6 July 1991 the Board of Physicians for the Congregation of the Causes of Saints unanimously accepted the cure of Sister Rubio. He was beatified on 17 May 1992.
</p><p>By way of a letter dated 15 March 1993, the Postulation for the Cause received news about the miraculous cure of Dr. Manuel Nevado Rey from cancerous chronic <a href="/wiki/Radiodermatitis" class="mw-redirect" title="Radiodermatitis">radiodermatitis</a>, an incurable disease, which took place in November 1992.<sup id="cite_ref-102" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-102">&#91;102&#93;</a></sup> The reported miracle, apparently brought about by Escrivá's intervention, was ruled valid by the Congregation for the Causes of Saints and approved by Pope John Paul II in December 2001, enabling the <a href="/wiki/Canonization" title="Canonization">canonization</a> of Escrivá. John Paul II, who frequently expressed public endorsement of Opus Dei and its work, canonized Escrivá on 6 October 2002.  The canonization Mass was attended by 42 cardinals and 470 bishops from around the world, general superiors of many orders and religious congregations, and representatives of various Catholic groups. During the days of the canonization event, church officials commented on the validity of the message of the founder, repeating John Paul II's decree <i>Christifideles Omnes</i> on Escrivá's virtues, which said that "by inviting Christians to be united to God through their daily work, which is something men will have to do and find their dignity in as long as the world lasts, the timeliness of this message is destined to endure as an inexhaustible source of spiritual light, regardless of changing epochs and situations."<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2011)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Criticism_of_the_process">Criticism of the process</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Josemar%C3%ADa_Escriv%C3%A1&amp;action=edit&amp;section=22" title="Edit section: Criticism of the process">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Various critics questioned the rapidity of <a href="/wiki/Josemar%C3%ADa_Escriv%C3%A1:_Canonisation#Criticism_about_process_of_canonization" class="mw-redirect" title="Josemaría Escrivá: Canonisation">Escrivá's canonization</a>. On the eve of Escrivá's beatification in 1992, journalist William D. Montalbano, writing for the <i><a href="/wiki/Los_Angeles_Times" title="Los Angeles Times">Los Angeles Times</a></i>, described it as "perhaps the most contentious beatification in modern times."<sup id="cite_ref-LATimes_103-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-LATimes-103">&#91;103&#93;</a></sup> Critics have argued that the process was plagued by irregularities. However, endorsers refer to Rafael Pérez, an <a href="/wiki/Augustinians" title="Augustinians">Augustinian</a> priest who presided over the tribunal in Madrid for Escrivá's cause, as "one of the best experts" on canonization. Pérez stated that the process was fast because Escrivá's figure is "of the universal importance," the Postulators "knew what they were doing", and, in 1983, the procedures were simplified in order to present "models who lived in a world like ours." Flavio Capucci, the postulator, also reported that the 6,000 postulatory letters to the Vatican showed "earnestness".<sup id="cite_ref-104" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-104">&#91;104&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Escrivá's canonization was one of the first to be processed after the <a href="/wiki/1983_Code_of_Canon_Law" title="1983 Code of Canon Law">1983 Code of Canon Law</a> streamlined the procedures for canonization, and so it was processed more quickly than was typical before. <a href="/wiki/Mother_Teresa" title="Mother Teresa">Mother Teresa</a> was canonized even more quickly, having been beatified just 6 years after her death (Escrivá was beatified in 17 years). According to journalist Kenneth L. Woodward, the 6,000-page long <i><a href="/wiki/Positio" title="Positio">positio</a></i> (the official document about the life and work of the candidate for sainthood prepared by the postulators) was declared confidential, but leaked to the press in 1992, after Escrivá's beatification. Woodward declared that, of 2,000 pages of testimonies, about 40% are by either <a href="/wiki/%C3%81lvaro_del_Portillo" title="Álvaro del Portillo">Álvaro del Portillo</a> or <a href="/wiki/Javier_Echevarr%C3%ADa_Rodr%C3%ADguez" title="Javier Echevarría Rodríguez">Javier Echevarría Rodríguez</a> who, as successors of Escrivá at the head of Opus Dei, would have the most to gain from the Catholic Church recognizing that organization's founder as a saint. The only critical testimony quoted in the <i>positio</i> was by Alberto Moncada, a Spanish sociologist who had been a member of Opus Dei and whose testimony might have been easier for the church authorities to dismiss because he had had little personal contact with Escrivá and had left the Catholic Church altogether. This critical testimony covered a mere two pages.<sup id="cite_ref-Woodward-Book-positio_105-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Woodward-Book-positio-105">&#91;105&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Critics of the process also questioned the fact that some of the physicians involved in the authentication of the two "scientifically inexplicable cures" achieved through the posthumous intercession of Escrivá, such as Dr. Raffaello Cortesini (a heart surgeon), were themselves members of Opus Dei.<sup id="cite_ref-106" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-106">&#91;106&#93;</a></sup> The Vatican has stated that the Medical Consultants for the Congregation affirmed unanimously that the miraculous cure of a cancerous state of chronic radiodermatitis in its third and irreversible stage in Dr. Manuel Nevado Rey (a country doctor in the village of <a href="/wiki/Almendralejo" title="Almendralejo">Almendralejo</a>) was "very quick, complete, lasting and scientifically inexplicable." After six months, the theological consultants, according to the Vatican, also unanimously attributed this cure to Escrivá.<sup id="cite_ref-107" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-107">&#91;107&#93;</a></sup> On the year of his canonization, the Opus Dei prelate reported that the Postulation has gathered 48 reports of unexplained medical favors attributed to Escriva's intercession, as well as 100,000 ordinary favours.<sup id="cite_ref-108" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-108">&#91;108&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Former Opus Dei members critical of Escrivá's character who claim that they were refused a hearing during the beatification and canonization processes include Miguel Fisac (a well-known Spanish architect who was one of the earliest members of Opus Dei and remained an associate of Escrivá for nearly twenty years),<sup id="cite_ref-Fisac1_29-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Fisac1-29">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Fisac2_109-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Fisac2-109">&#91;109&#93;</a></sup> Vladimir Felzmann (a Czech-born engineer and Catholic priest from the UK, who was Escrivá's personal assistant),<sup id="cite_ref-Woodward_46-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Woodward-46">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Thompson_65-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Thompson-65">&#91;65&#93;</a></sup> María del Carmen Tapia (who worked with Escrivá in Opus Dei's central offices in Rome and directed its printing press),<sup id="cite_ref-110" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-110">&#91;110&#93;</a></sup> Carlos Albás (a Spanish lawyer who was also Escrivá's first cousin once removed),<sup id="cite_ref-111" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-111">&#91;111&#93;</a></sup> María Angustias Moreno (who was an official of the women's part of Opus Dei, during Escrivá's lifetime),<sup id="cite_ref-112" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-112">&#91;112&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-113" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-113">&#91;113&#93;</a></sup> and Dr. John Roche (an Irish physicist and historian of science who was a member of Opus Dei from 1959 to 1973, and managed one of its schools in Kenya).<sup id="cite_ref-114" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-114">&#91;114&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-115" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-115">&#91;115&#93;</a></sup> Several groups critical of Escrivá and of Opus Dei emerged both before and after the canonization of Escrivá, including the Opus Dei Awareness Network (ODAN),<sup id="cite_ref-116" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-116">&#91;116&#93;</a></sup> and "OpusLibros",<sup id="cite_ref-117" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-117">&#91;117&#93;</a></sup> both collaborations of former members who now oppose Opus Dei and its practices.
</p><p>According to journalist Kenneth L. Woodward, before the official beatification he
</p>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r996844942"/><blockquote class="templatequote"><p>was able to interview six other men and women who had lived and/or worked closely with Escrivá.  The examples they gave of vanity, venality, temper tantrums, harshness toward subordinates, and criticism of popes and other churchmen were hardly the characteristics one expects to find in a Christian saint.  But their testimony was not allowed to be heard.  At least two of them were vilified in the <i>positio</i> by name, yet neither of them was permitted to defend their reputations.<sup id="cite_ref-Woodward-Book-critics_118-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Woodward-Book-critics-118">&#91;118&#93;</a></sup></p></blockquote>
<p>Catholic theologian <a href="/wiki/Richard_McBrien" title="Richard McBrien">Richard McBrien</a> termed Escrivá's sainthood "the most blatant example of a politicized [canonization] in modern times."<sup id="cite_ref-119" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-119">&#91;119&#93;</a></sup> According to Catholic writer and biographer John Allen such views are countered by many other ex-members, the present members, and the estimated 900,000 people who attend activities of Opus Dei. He says that the interpretation of the facts "seems to depend upon one's basic approach to spirituality, family life, and the implications of a religious vocation."  Allen's account of Opus Dei and its founder, however, was not accepted by all reviewers as impartial.<sup id="cite_ref-Thompson-Allen_78-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Thompson-Allen-78">&#91;78&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Reports_of_discord_among_judges">Reports of discord among judges</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Josemar%C3%ADa_Escriv%C3%A1&amp;action=edit&amp;section=23" title="Edit section: Reports of discord among judges">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Escrivá's canonization attracted an unusual amount of attention and criticism, both within the Catholic Church and by the press. Flavio Capucci, the <a href="/wiki/Postulator" title="Postulator">postulator</a> of Escrivá's cause for sainthood, summarized the main accusations against Escrivá: that "he had a bad temper, that he was cruel, that he was vain, that he was close to Spanish dictator Francisco Franco, that he was pro-<a href="/wiki/Nazi" class="mw-redirect" title="Nazi">Nazi</a> and that he was so dismayed by the <a href="/wiki/Second_Vatican_Council" title="Second Vatican Council">Second Vatican Council</a> that he even travelled to <a href="/wiki/Greece" title="Greece">Greece</a> with the idea that he might convert to the <a href="/wiki/Eastern_Orthodox_Church" title="Eastern Orthodox Church">Orthodox</a> religion".<sup id="cite_ref-Woodward_46-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Woodward-46">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-120" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-120">&#91;120&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>A <i><a href="/wiki/Newsweek" title="Newsweek">Newsweek</a></i> article by Woodward claimed that, of the nine judges of the <a href="/wiki/Congregation_for_the_Causes_of_Saints" class="mw-redirect" title="Congregation for the Causes of Saints">Congregation for the Causes of Saints</a> presiding over Escrivá's cause for beatification, two had requested a suspension of the proceedings. The dissenters were identified as <a href="/wiki/Luigi_De_Magistris_(cardinal)" title="Luigi De Magistris (cardinal)">Luigi De Magistris</a>, a prelate working in the Vatican's tribunal of the <a href="/wiki/Apostolic_Penitentiary" title="Apostolic Penitentiary">Apostolic Penitentiary</a>, and Justo Fernández Alonso, rector of the Spanish National Church in <a href="/wiki/Rome" title="Rome">Rome</a>.  According to Woodward, one of the dissenters wrote that the beatification of Escrivá could cause the church "grave public scandal."<sup id="cite_ref-Woodward_46-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Woodward-46">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup> The same article quoted Cardinal <a href="/wiki/Silvio_Oddi" title="Silvio Oddi">Silvio Oddi</a> as declaring that many bishops were "very displeased" with the rush to canonize Escrivá so soon after his death.<sup id="cite_ref-Woodward_46-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Woodward-46">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup> In interviews, <a href="/wiki/Jos%C3%A9_Saraiva_Martins" title="José Saraiva Martins">José Saraiva Martins</a>, Cardinal Prefect of the <a href="/wiki/Congregation_for_the_Causes_of_Saints" class="mw-redirect" title="Congregation for the Causes of Saints">Congregation for the Causes of Saints</a>, has denied being aware of that dissent.<sup id="cite_ref-121" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-121">&#91;121&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The journal <i>Il Regno</i>, published in <a href="/wiki/Bologna" title="Bologna">Bologna</a> by the congregation of the <a href="/wiki/Leon_Dehon" class="mw-redirect" title="Leon Dehon">Priests of the Sacred Heart</a> (the Dehonians), reproduced, in May 1992, the confidential vote of one of the judges in Escrivá's cause of beatification, in which the judge asked that the process be suspended.  The document questioned the haste of the proceedings, the near absence of testimony from critics in the documentation gathered by the postulators, the failure of the documentation to properly address issues about Escrivá's relations with the Franco regime and with other Catholic organizations, and suggestions from the official testimonies themselves that Escrivá lacked proper spiritual humility.<sup id="cite_ref-VoteSuspension_97-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-VoteSuspension-97">&#91;97&#93;</a></sup>  This document does not identify the judge by name, but its author indicates that he met Escrivá only once, briefly, in 1966, while serving as a notary for the <a href="/wiki/Congregation_for_the_Doctrine_of_the_Faith" class="mw-redirect" title="Congregation for the Doctrine of the Faith">Holy Office</a>, which implies that the judge in question was De Magistris.
</p><p>As regent of the Apostolic Penitentiary, at the time of the vote De Magistris's work was largely concerned with issues arising from confession and penance.  According to church law, a confessor has an absolute duty not to disclose anything that he might have learned from a penitent in the course of a confession (see <a href="/wiki/Seal_of_the_Confessional_in_the_Catholic_Church" class="mw-redirect" title="Seal of the Confessional in the Catholic Church">Seal of the Confessional in the Catholic Church</a>).  In his vote, which its own contents date to August 1989, De Magistris argued that the testimony from the main witness, <a href="/wiki/%C3%81lvaro_del_Portillo" title="Álvaro del Portillo">Álvaro del Portillo</a>, should have been entirely excluded from the proceedings, since Portillo had been Escrivá's <a href="/wiki/Sacrament_of_Penance_(Catholic_Church)" class="mw-redirect" title="Sacrament of Penance (Catholic Church)">confessor</a> for 31 years.<sup id="cite_ref-VoteSuspension_97-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-VoteSuspension-97">&#91;97&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>John Allen Jr. comments that, according to some observers within the Catholic Church, De Magistris was punished for his opposition to Escrivá's canonization. De Magistris was promoted in 2001 to the head of the Apostolic Penitentiary, an important position in the Vatican bureaucracy usually occupied by a <a href="/wiki/Cardinal_(Catholicism)" class="mw-redirect" title="Cardinal (Catholicism)">cardinal</a>.  However, Pope John Paul II did not make De Magistris a cardinal and replaced him as head of the Apostolic Penitentiary after less than two years, effectively forcing him into retirement.<sup id="cite_ref-122" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-122">&#91;122&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-123" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-123">&#91;123&#93;</a></sup>  The decision by <a href="/wiki/Pope_Francis" title="Pope Francis">Pope Francis</a> to make De Magistris a cardinal at the consistory of 14 February 2015, when De Magistris was about to turn 89 and therefore could no longer participate in <a href="/wiki/Papal_conclave" title="Papal conclave">papal conclaves</a>, was interpreted by some commentators as consolation for how De Magistris had been treated under John Paul II.<sup id="cite_ref-mcdermott_124-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-mcdermott-124">&#91;124&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Teachings_and_legacy">Teachings and legacy</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Josemar%C3%ADa_Escriv%C3%A1&amp;action=edit&amp;section=24" title="Edit section: Teachings and legacy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Escrivas_plaque.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/80/Escrivas_plaque.jpg/220px-Escrivas_plaque.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="293" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/80/Escrivas_plaque.jpg/330px-Escrivas_plaque.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/80/Escrivas_plaque.jpg/440px-Escrivas_plaque.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3240" data-file-height="4320" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Escrivas_plaque.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Bronze portrait of Escrivá in <a href="/wiki/Cahir" title="Cahir">Cahir</a>, Ireland, commemorating a visit he made in 1959.</div></div></div>
<p>The significance of Escrivá's message and teachings has been a topic of debate, by Catholics and others.  The Protestant French historian <a href="/wiki/Pierre_Chaunu" title="Pierre Chaunu">Pierre Chaunu</a>, a professor at the Sorbonne and president of the Academy of Moral and Political Sciences, said that "the work of Escrivá de Balaguer will undoubtedly mark the 21st century. This is a prudent and reasonable wager. Do not pass close to this contemporary without paying him close attention".<sup id="cite_ref-125" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-125">&#91;125&#93;</a></sup> The Catholic theologian <a href="/wiki/Hans_Urs_von_Balthasar" title="Hans Urs von Balthasar">Hans Urs von Balthasar</a>, who was appointed <a href="/wiki/Cardinal_(Catholicism)" class="mw-redirect" title="Cardinal (Catholicism)">cardinal</a> by Pope John Paul II (but died in 1988 before his investiture), dismissed Escrivá's principal work, <i>The Way</i>, as "a little Spanish manual for advanced Boy Scouts" and argued that it was quite insufficient to sustain a major religious organization. However, the monk and spiritual writer <a href="/wiki/Thomas_Merton" title="Thomas Merton">Thomas Merton</a> declared that Escrivá's book "will certainly do a great deal of good by its simplicity, which is the true medium for the Gospel message".<sup id="cite_ref-Allen_2005_64_39-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Allen_2005_64-39">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Critics of Opus Dei have often argued that the importance and originality of Escrivá's intellectual contributions to theology, history, and law, at least as measured by his published writings, has been grossly exaggerated by his devotees.<sup id="cite_ref-Allen_2005_64_39-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Allen_2005_64-39">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup> However, various officials of the Catholic Church have spoken well of Escrivá's influence and of the relevance of his teachings. In the decree introducing the cause of beatification and canonization of Escrivá, Cardinal <a href="/wiki/Ugo_Poletti" title="Ugo Poletti">Ugo Poletti</a> wrote in 1981: "For having proclaimed the <a href="/wiki/Universal_call_to_holiness" title="Universal call to holiness">universal call to holiness</a> since he founded Opus Dei during 1928, Msgr. Josemaría Escrivá de Balaguer, has been unanimously recognized as the precursor of precisely what constitutes the fundamental nucleus of the Church's magisterium, a message of such fruitfulness in the life of the Church."<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2011)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> <a href="/wiki/Sebastiano_Baggio" title="Sebastiano Baggio">Sebastiano Baggio</a>, Cardinal Prefect of the <a href="/wiki/Congregation_for_Bishops" class="mw-redirect" title="Congregation for Bishops">Congregation for Bishops</a>, wrote a month after Escrivá's death: "It is evident even today that the life, works, and message of the founder of Opus Dei constitutes a turning point, or more exactly a new original chapter in the history of Christian spirituality." A Vatican <i>peritus</i> or consultor for the process of beatification said that "he is like a figure from the deepest spiritual sources". <a href="/wiki/Franz_K%C3%B6nig" title="Franz König">Franz König</a>, Archbishop of Vienna, wrote in 1975:
</p><p>"The magnetic force of Opus Dei probably comes from its profoundly lay spirituality. At the very beginning, in 1928, Msgr. Escrivá anticipated the return to the Patrimony of the Church brought by the Second Vatican Council ... [H]e was able to anticipate the great themes of the Church's pastoral action in the dawn of the third millennium of her history."<sup id="cite_ref-126" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-126">&#91;126&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-127" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-127">&#91;127&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The "absolutely central" part of Escrivá's teaching, says American theologian William May, is that "sanctification is possible only because of the grace of God, freely given to his children through his only-begotten Son, and it consists essentially in an intimate, loving union with Jesus, our Redeemer and Savior."<sup id="cite_ref-128" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-128">&#91;128&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Escrivá's books, including <i>Furrow</i>, <i>The Way</i>, <i>Christ is Passing By</i>, and <i>The Forge</i>, continue to be read widely, and emphasize the laity's calling to daily sanctification (a message also to be found in the documents of <a href="/wiki/Vatican_II" class="mw-redirect" title="Vatican II">Vatican II</a>). Pope John Paul II made the following observation in his homily at the beatification of Escrivá:
</p>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r996844942"/><blockquote class="templatequote"><p>With supernatural intuition, Blessed Josemaría untiringly preached the universal call to holiness and apostolate. Christ calls everyone to become holy in the realities of everyday life. Hence work too is a means of personal holiness and apostolate, when it is done in union with Jesus Christ.</p></blockquote>
<p>John Paul II's decree <i>Christifideles omnes</i> states: "By inviting Christians to seek union with God through their daily work&#160;— which confers dignity on human beings and is their lot as long as they exist on earth&#160;— his message is destined to endure as an inexhaustible source of spiritual light regardless of changing epochs and situations".<sup id="cite_ref-ACF_16-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ACF-16">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Writings">Writings</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Josemar%C3%ADa_Escriv%C3%A1&amp;action=edit&amp;section=25" title="Edit section: Writings">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>

<li id="cite_note-56"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-56">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"><a href="#CITEREFEscrivá1987">Escrivá 1987</a>, Maxim 315 <i>"Love your own country: it is a Christian virtue to be patriotic. But if patriotism becomes nationalism, which leads you to look at other people, at other countries, with indifference, with scorn, without Christian charity and justice, then it is a sin."</i> <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="http://www.escrivaworks.org/book/furrow-point-315.htm">Furrow</a></span>

And at the same time, to hold as your own the noble aspirations of other lands.&#160;— So many glories of France are glories of mine! And in the same way, much that makes Germans proud, and the peoples of Italy and of England..., and Americans and Asians and Africans, is a source of pride to me also. Catholic: big heart, broad mind."</i> <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="http://www.escrivaworks.org/book/the_way-point-525.htm">Excerpt from <i>The Way</i></a></span>

<dl><dt>Opus Dei members</dt></dl>
<ul><li><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1133582631"/><cite id="CITEREFVázquez_de_Prada2001" class="citation cs2"><a href="/wiki/Andr%C3%A9s_V%C3%A1zquez_de_Prada" title="Andrés Vázquez de Prada">Vázquez de Prada, Andrés</a> (2001), <i>The Founder of Opus Dei: the Life of Josemaría Escrivá</i>, Princeton: Scepter Publishers, <a href="/wiki/ISBN_(identifier)" class="mw-redirect" title="ISBN (identifier)">ISBN</a>&#160;<a href="/wiki/Special:BookSources/978-1-889334-25-7" title="Special:BookSources/978-1-889334-25-7"><bdi>978-1-889334-25-7</bdi></a></cite><span title="ctx_ver=Z39.88-2004&amp;rft_val_fmt=info%3Aofi%2Ffmt%3Akev%3Amtx%3Abook&amp;rft.genre=book&amp;rft.btitle=The+Founder+of+Opus+Dei%3A+the+Life+of+Josemar%C3%ADa+Escriv%C3%A1&amp;rft.place=Princeton&amp;rft.pub=Scepter+Publishers&amp;rft.date=2001&amp;rft.isbn=978-1-889334-25-7&amp;rft.aulast=V%C3%A1zquez+de+Prada&amp;rft.aufirst=Andr%C3%A9s&amp;rfr_id=info%3Asid%2Fen.wikipedia.org%3AJosemar%C3%ADa+Escriv%C3%A1" class="Z3988"></span></li>
<li><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1133582631"/><cite id="CITEREFBelda,_Manuel1997" class="citation cs2">Belda, Manuel, ed. (1997), <i>Holiness and the World: Studies in the Teachings of Blessed Josemariá Escrivá</i>, Princeton: Scepter Publications, <a href="/wiki/ISBN_(identifier)" class="mw-redirect" title="ISBN (identifier)">ISBN</a>&#160;<a href="/wiki/Special:BookSources/1-890177-04-0" title="Special:BookSources/1-890177-04-0"><bdi>1-890177-04-0</bdi></a></cite><span title="ctx_ver=Z39.88-2004&amp;rft_val_fmt=info%3Aofi%2Ffmt%3Akev%3Amtx%3Abook&amp;rft.genre=book&amp;rft.btitle=Holiness+and+the+World%3A+Studies+in+the+Teachings+of+Blessed+Josemari%C3%A1+Escriv%C3%A1&amp;rft.place=Princeton&amp;rft.pub=Scepter+Publications&amp;rft.date=1997&amp;rft.isbn=1-890177-04-0&amp;rfr_id=info%3Asid%2Fen.wikipedia.org%3AJosemar%C3%ADa+Escriv%C3%A1" class="Z3988"></span> Collection of contributions to a theological symposium; contributors include Ratzinger, del Portillo, Cottier, dalla Torre, Ocariz, Illanes, Aranda, Burggraf and an address by Pope John Paul II.</li>
<li><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1133582631"/><cite id="CITEREFBerglar1994" class="citation cs2">Berglar, Peter (1994), <i>Opus Dei. Life and Work of its Founder</i>, Princeton: Scepter Publishers, <a href="/wiki/ISBN_(identifier)" class="mw-redirect" title="ISBN (identifier)">ISBN</a>&#160;<a href="/wiki/Special:BookSources/0-933932-65-0" title="Special:BookSources/0-933932-65-0"><bdi>0-933932-65-0</bdi></a></cite><span title="ctx_ver=Z39.88-2004&amp;rft_val_fmt=info%3Aofi%2Ffmt%3Akev%3Amtx%3Abook&amp;rft.genre=book&amp;rft.btitle=Opus+Dei.+Life+and+Work+of+its+Founder&amp;rft.place=Princeton&amp;rft.pub=Scepter+Publishers&amp;rft.date=1994&amp;rft.isbn=0-933932-65-0&amp;rft.aulast=Berglar&amp;rft.aufirst=Peter&amp;rfr_id=info%3Asid%2Fen.wikipedia.org%3AJosemar%C3%ADa+Escriv%C3%A1" class="Z3988"></span>. A study of Opus Dei based on the life story and work of its founder written by a professor of history at the University of Cologne.</li>

The miracle was the cure of Dr. Manuel Nevado from cancerous chronic radiodermatitis, an incurable disease, which took place in November 1992. The decree opened the doors for the canonization of Blessed Josemaría.</q></cite><span title="ctx_ver=Z39.88-2004&amp;rft_val_fmt=info%3Aofi%2Ffmt%3Akev%3Amtx%3Abook&amp;rft.genre=book&amp;rft.btitle=Josemar%C3%ADa+Escriv%C3%A1+de+Balaguer+%E2%80%94+The+Miracle+Approved+for+the+Canonization&amp;rft.place=Vatican&amp;rft.pub=Holy+See&amp;rft.date=2001-09-21&amp;rft.au=Sacred+Congregation+for+the+Causes+of+Saints&amp;rft_id=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.vatican.va%2Flatest%2Fdocuments%2Fescriva_miracolo-canoniz_en.html&amp;rfr_id=info%3Asid%2Fen.wikipedia.org%3AJosemar%C3%ADa+Escriv%C3%A1" class="Z3988"></span></li></ul>
